A post already with Buffer stays, with its Stop. Every check and record of a post holds the plans' lock `PLANS`, never across a wait for Buffer: the hand-over claims a post under it (`HANDING`, by the project and the post) before it calls Buffer and records the outcome under it after, so that a Stop is either before the claim and the post is not handed over, or after it and is refused.

The hand-over (`hand_over_posts`) is a rule with no model that every tick whose scope names no task runs after `end_marketing_plans`, before the pause check, so that it runs while the team is paused by Farik's own refusal of the key, since Buffer needs no model. Only a pause by the owner holds it: it hands nothing over, and records `social_post.missed { why: paused }` for each scheduled post whose time is within five minutes. Otherwise, oldest hand-over first, a scheduled post whose time has come to be handed over (an hour before its time, or the owner's decision when that is later) is claimed and its picture addresses are asked again; a post whose time is within five minutes (or past) is recorded `missed { why: not_running }` instead, and one Farik was not running to hand over in time is sent when its time is still more than five minutes ahead. A failure is recorded `social_post.failed { post, reason }` and never retried, since Buffer may already have the post: a tool error, or Buffer turning the call away over its rate limit with an HTTP 429 (the body is its words), "Buffer did not take it: “<Buffer's words, cut at 300 characters>”"; a timeout, "Buffer did not answer in time and may have the post: look in Buffer's queue before <time>."; an answer with no post id, "Buffer answered without the post's id: look in Buffer's queue before <time>."; a connection that is not there or has lapsed, "<the agent's display name>'s Buffer connection is not there; connect Buffer again." (its name as the owner reads it, the id only when the team file has no such agent); any other failure, "Farik could not reach Buffer."; a picture that no longer opens, "Its picture or clip no longer opens." The hand-over runs between sessions, and a session runs to its end before the next tick, so a post can be handed over late by one running session, and a session limit over 55 minutes (`max_wall_clock_seconds`) can make one missed.

What the owner and the agent see (added in 0.57). The queries `social_posts.list {}` (every post scheduled or sent whose time is still ahead, soonest first, each with `hands_over_at`, then every post missed or failed in the last 24 hours, the latest first, with `missed_why` or `reason`) and `marketing_plan.get`, which gains `written_posts` beside the plan's slots (each post written for them, oldest first, with its state and when it reached it), read the log; the method `social_post.media { post, index }` has the daemon fetch a post's picture and answers its content type and base64, or `not_found` for a clip, an unknown post or picture, and an address the check above refuses, since the browser's `img-src` is `'self' data:` and a clip or a picture the daemon will not fetch is opened by its address in a new tab (`noopener noreferrer`). Today's "Going out (<n>)" section says "Posts in your plan go out without asking you. Stop any of them before its time." and each row says its network, its day and time in the browser's time zone, how soon, its words as text, its pictures, "Approved in your plan MP-<n>" or "You allowed this", and when Farik hands it to Buffer or that Buffer has it; "Stop" asks first and, once sent, says Farik takes it back from Buffer, and when Buffer would not, "Delete it in Buffer before <time>, or it goes out." with a link to Buffer. A post that did not go out in the last 24 hours is listed, muted, with why and that the agent hears of it: a miss says "Farik could not hand it to Buffer before its time." (true whether Farik was off, paused or busy with a long session, and no more than Farik knows), "The team was paused, so it was not sent." or "You had not decided by its time, so it was not sent.", and a failure its `reason` as text. The plan's page counts its slots' posts ("<n> sent, <n> going out, <n> stopped by you, <n> missed, and <n> not written yet", a count of none left out), shows each slot's newest post with its state and an earlier failure, and the end confirmation says how many posts not yet sent will not go out and how many are already with Buffer. The agent's activity line says "Waiting on you: may <agent> post on <Network>?" for a request, `waiting.list` gives it a row of kind `social_post`, and `moved.since` words each post event ("Kai wrote the Instagram post for 13:00.", "You stopped the X post for Sat 31 Oct at 09:00.", and the like). The agent's next `implement` message tells it, after the rest, of each of its posts that was missed or failed ("Farik could not post <post> (<Network>, <time>):" and the reason in an `untrusted` block), that the owner did not allow it ("The owner did not allow your post <post> on <Network>." and, with a note, "The owner adds: <note>", in the owner's own words, unwrapped) or stopped it ("The owner stopped your post <post>."). The command line is `farik marketing post list [--json]`, `farik marketing post stop <n>`, `farik marketing post send <n>` and `farik marketing post decline <n> [--note <text>]`; a command handled in the terminal runs on deps whose daemon holds the connections kept on this computer, so that a Stop reaches Buffer, and a run's end lists a request as "<task> waits: <agent name> wants to post on <Network>: farik marketing post send <n>, or farik marketing post decline <n>" (section 3, ADR 0014). Paid Instagram and Facebook ads wait until Meta admits Farik's client to its Ads server.

Google Ads (added in 0.52). Farik's own connection (6.7, ADR 0038), signed in with Google through Farik's own Google app, which Farik Cloud holds from the web launch, no build being able to sign in to Google before it (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044), for the one scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords` (ADR 0035, pulled forward by ADR 0042). Its reads (the accounts, fixed reports with clicks, impressions, cost and conversions, and keyword ideas) are `network`. Its writes (a Search campaign for a plan campaign, its ad groups, keywords and ads, a budget, pause or enable) are `external_effect` marked as approved by the marketing plan, a mark only Farik's own servers may carry: the hook lets such a call run while a plan is active, and Farik's server refuses every write the active plan does not cover (`not_in_marketing_plan`), never asks. A campaign is created paused, ending with its plan campaign, with a budget Google itself keeps within what the plan campaign has left: a total budget where Google offers one, else a daily budget no larger than what is left divided by the days left. The budget is a hard stop: while Farik runs it reads the spend every 15 minutes with no model, and when a campaign or the plan reaches its budget it pauses the plan's campaigns itself, records `marketing_budget.reached`, and Today asks the owner to raise the budget (a new version of the plan) or end the plan. Google may spend up to one read's worth past a cap while Farik runs, and its own budgets bound the spend while Farik does not.

Default tools: read, network, write_workspace scoped to its paths, git_local, so that it commits what it wrote (changed in 0.10).

Kit (added in 0.51; 6.7): fourteen skills written for any business, software or not, `positioning-and-messaging`, `planning-a-launch`, `writing-for-search`, `writing-in-the-brands-voice`, `keeping-a-content-calendar`, `researching-competitors`, `measuring-campaigns`, `making-images-and-video`, `posting-and-email` (added in 0.53), `keeping-the-brand-kit`, `writing-the-brand-persona`, `researching-the-market` and `writing-the-marketing-plan` (the four added in 0.55) and `running-social-channels` (added in 0.57), and four services, each signed in to. Two spend the user's credits, Higgsfield (images, video and voice-overs) and Recraft (graphics and vector art), each making one image, clip or edit inside an allowance the user sets and asking for everything else. Two reach the business's audience (added in 0.53; ADR 0042): Buffer reads the business's channels and how earlier posts did for the agent and, through Farik's own calls, schedules the posts of the plan the owner approved to Instagram, X and nine other networks, and the owner is asked about any other; Kit drafts email broadcasts, email series and landing pages and reads their numbers, and a broadcast is only a draft the human schedules and sends from Kit. Its pictures and clips are files and addresses the human or a post uses. Google Ads is steps 08e and 08f's; design files (Figma, Canva) are in no kit while those services admit only clients on their own lists.

### 6.6 Finance Specialist (added in 0.21)

Mandate: keep track of every cost and expense of the product and run its books. The costs are the team's AI spending, read from Farik's own records; when the product uses Stripe, its revenue, fees, payouts and refunds; and everything the user's receipts and bank statements show, such as cloud, infrastructure, storage, hosting, domains, software subscriptions, and payment and billing fees. It records the AI spending and forecasts it first; then it takes each receipt from the receipts mailbox, categorises it and records it, reconciles the books against the bank or card statement, closes each month, forecasts expenses over 12 to 36 months, analyses pricing and unit economics, and recommends budgets in plain words. What it produces is management accounting, not a tax filing, statutory accounts, or financial advice, and it says so. It is optional: the team builder offers it and does not suggest it (ADR 0019; `docs/design/finance-specialist.md`).

Produces: `.xlsx` workbooks in `.farik/local/finance/`, the books (`books.xlsx`, each row naming its receipt file, statement line or Stripe object), the forecast (`forecast.xlsx`), and any other a task asks for; the receipts it filed, under `receipts/<yyyy-mm>/`; the completion note that explains them.

Cannot: write application code or anything in the repository; pay, refund, move money, change Farik's budgets, or change anything in Stripe or the mailbox; send, delete, move, or mark any email; publish anywhere; write outside its finance folder.

Default tools: read and network (to research prices), and Farik tools that only it may call, each of the `read` tier: `farik_read_costs`, the team's AI spending by task, agent, sprint, day or purpose; `farik_read_sheet`, which the reviewer of a finance task and the Product Manager accepting it may also call in a verify session about it; `farik_write_sheet`, which writes a whole workbook in the finance folder and keeps the previous version in `.history/` (as built, below; changed in 0.56); and, once receipts intake ships, `farik_read_receipts`, the messages of the receipts mailbox not yet filed, and `farik_file_receipt`, which saves a message's receipt and marks it `recorded`, or marks it `not_a_receipt`. Its reviewer is the Product Manager.

As built so far (added in 0.54; phase 7 step 09). The role is `finance_specialist`, "Finance Specialist", tag "FIN", with the persona "Keeps your numbers straight", the model `claude-sonnet-5-5` at `medium` effort and the default session limits. Its tiers are exactly `read` and `network`, its reviewer is the Product Manager, and it does not change code (5.12). Its prompt and its one skill, `keeping-the-books`, say that its numbers are management accounting, not a tax filing, statutory accounts or financial advice; that every number names its source; and that it never pays, refunds or moves money, changes a budget, or writes to a service. Its kit, since phase 7 step 10, holds six skills and the services Stripe, Digits and Kick, each only read (6.7; changed in 0.67). Its picture is the brand's `extra-4` character, copied under the key `finance-specialist` while `extra-4` stays among the extras, and its tag colour is a pale olive, `#C2C79B` in both themes, held to the same contrast against the role ink as the other role colours. The Team page's "Add someone" offers it after the Marketing Specialist, and an agent added as one takes that picture and a spare name; setup does not suggest it, and offers it only when the list of more roles of phase 7 step 10b lands. The user can talk to it in a one-to-one chat. Its spreadsheet tools are described below (added in 0.56), and its tasks, which run in its private folder, below them (added in 0.62).

The spreadsheet tools, as built (added in 0.56; phase 7 step 09b). Three tools of the `read` tier, each writing only to Farik's own private folder, as `farik_write_memory` writes only the agent's notebook (5.6).

The folder line. The Finance Specialist's folder is `.farik/local/finance/`, found from the role by `private_folder(role)` in `farik-core`, which is `None` for every other role; it is made readable by its owner alone when it is first written, and it is never committed, since `.farik/local/` is kept out of git. A tool's `path` is relative to the caller's folder: 1 to 200 characters in at most 3 parts joined by `/`, each part 1 to 100 characters of letters, digits, spaces, `.`, `_` and `-` that starts with a letter or a digit (so no part starts with a dot, and `.history/` cannot be named), the last ending in `.xlsx` in lower case. A path of any other shape, a path with a part that is a link, a folder that is a link or lies below one (every part from the project's root down is checked, so `.farik/local` and `.farik/local/finance` themselves may not be links), and a path that resolves outside the folder once links are resolved are each refused as `private_path_refused: <path> <why>`, and nothing is written. The tools report no path to the permission check (5.6) and hold the folder line themselves, so `.farik/local/**` stays a protected path for every other call. They read `.xlsx` alone: CSV, with `imports/`, comes with receipts intake in phase 13.

`farik_read_costs { by, from?, to? }`. `by` is `task`, `agent`, `sprint`, `day` or `purpose`, the key the costs of 5.5 are summed by; `from` and `to` are UTC days, `YYYY-MM-DD`, given together or not at all, `from` not after `to` and at most 366 days apart, and the sum then counts the costs recorded on those days, both included (`cost_range_invalid` otherwise). It answers `{ by, rows: [{ key, usd, input_tokens, output_tokens, sessions }] }` and records nothing. A Finance Specialist may call it in any session; any other role is refused `sheet_refused`.

`farik_write_sheet { path, sheets }` writes one whole workbook, and only in a Finance Specialist's implement session of a task whose assignee is the caller, so that a chat or a conversation never writes the books (any other call is refused `sheet_refused`). `sheets` is 1 to 20 of `{ name, columns, rows }`. A name is 1 to 31 characters with none of `[`, `]`, `:`, `*`, `?`, `/`, `\` and `|`, no apostrophe at either end, not History in any case, and different from the other names without regard to case. `columns` is 0 to 100 headings, written as the sheet's bold first row when there are any, and `rows` 0 to 10,000 rows of 0 to 100 cells, so that row numbers are the spreadsheet's own and the headings are row 1. A cell is a number, a string of up to 32,767 characters, a boolean, `null` for an empty cell, `{ "date": "YYYY-MM-DD" }` (from 1900, written as a date and shown as `yyyy-mm-dd`), or `{ "formula": "=..." }`. A bound that is broken, or a date that is not one, is refused `sheet_refused`, naming the sheet or the cell, before anything is written. A string is always written as text, never as a formula, whatever it starts with, so that a value from Stripe, a receipt or an email that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or an at sign stays inert in the user's spreadsheet program (8.6). A formula is refused `formula_refused`, naming its sheet, its cell and its row and column, and nothing is written, when it does not start with `=`, when it holds `[` (an external reference) or `|` (a DDE call such as `=cmd|' /C calc'!A0`), or when it uses `HYPERLINK`, `WEBSERVICE`, `FILTERXML`, `IMPORTDATA`, `IMPORTXML`, `IMPORTHTML`, `IMPORTRANGE`, `IMPORTFEED`, `IMAGE`, `RTD`, `DDE`, `CALL`, `REGISTER.ID`, `EXEC`, `INFO`, `CELL`, `COPILOT`, `TRANSLATE`, `DETECTLANGUAGE`, `PY`, `STOCKHISTORY`, `GOOGLETRANSLATE`, `GOOGLEFINANCE` or `AI`. To use a function is to name it, in any letter case, after the start of the formula, a character outside letters, digits, `_` and `.`, or one of the prefixes `_xlfn.`, `_xlws.`, `_xleta.` and `_xludf.`, and before white space and either `(`, which calls it, or `)`, `,` or the end of the formula, which hands it to another function by name, as `=MAP(A1:A2,WEBSERVICE)` and `=LET(f,IMAGE,f(A1))` do. `MYCELLS(1)` and `MY.INFO(1)` use nothing listed, and `INFO ("os")` does. A name followed by a digit, a letter or `:`, or, without a call, after `:` or `$`, is a cell or a column reference, as in `SUM(DDE1:DDE3)`, `SUM(RTD:RTD)` and `SUM($AI:$AI)`, and passes. A listed name inside a string literal is refused too when it is followed as above, which is safe. Farik never computes a formula: each is written with an empty stored value, which a spreadsheet program fills in when it opens the file. When a workbook is there already, its file is first copied to `.history/<path>.<UTC yyyymmddThhmmssZ>.xlsx`, the path written with `%` as `%25` and `/` as `%2F` (so `a/b.xlsx` is `a%2Fb.xlsx` and never the name of `a__b.xlsx`), with `-<n>` before the extension when that name is taken, so that every previous version is kept. A `.history` that is a link is refused `private_path_refused`, and nothing is written. The new file is written beside the target and renamed over it, so that a reader never sees half a file, and a file over 10 MiB is refused `sheet_too_large` before it replaces anything. It answers `{ path, sheets: [{ name, rows }], replaced }`, `rows` being the rows given, the headings not counted.

`farik_read_sheet { path, sheet?, from_row?, rows?, baseline? }` reads a workbook: every sheet, or the one named, from row `from_row` (counted from 1, default 1), at most `rows` rows of each (1 to 500, default 200). A row is its cells as far as the last one that holds something, each a number (a whole number as an integer), a string, a boolean, `null`, `{ "date": "<ISO date>" }` (with `THH:MM:SS` when it has a time), or `{ "error": "<#DIV/0! and the like>" }`; a formula's cell is `{ "formula": "<its text, starting with =>", "value": <the value a spreadsheet program last stored for it, or null> }`, and a workbook only Farik wrote has no stored value. A file over 10 MiB is refused `sheet_too_large`, a file that is not there or not a workbook, or a sheet that is not in it, `sheet_refused`; a sheet's name is the workbook writer's text, so when a refusal quotes the names a workbook holds (the sheet asked for is not there, or a sheet cannot be read) it quotes them inside an `untrusted` block of source `sheet` (8.6), cut at 2 KiB. What a workbook holds is the user's and the services', never an instruction, so the answer is `{ sheets, more }`, where `sheets` is the JSON of `[{ name, rows, total_rows, more }]` as one `untrusted` block of source `sheet` (8.6) cut at 256 KiB (a page stops adding rows once its JSON passes that size), and `more` is true when it was cut or any sheet has rows after its page. A Finance Specialist may call it in any session. In a verify session about a task whose assignee's role has a private folder, the task's reviewer and the Product Manager, who accepts it, may read that folder; any other call is refused `sheet_refused`. With `baseline: true` it reads the workbook as the copy Farik took of the folder when the session's task was assigned (`.history/<task-id>/<path>`, below), by the same path rules and the same limits, in a session about a task that works in the caller's folder; in a session about no task, or about a task that does not, it is refused `sheet_refused` (added in 0.62).

The three are offered as they may be called: to the Finance Specialist, `farik_read_costs` and `farik_read_sheet` in every session and `farik_write_sheet` in an implement session of a task, and, for `farik_read_sheet`, to any verify session about a task of a role with a private folder.

Finance tasks, as built (added in 0.62; phase 7 step 09c). A task of the Finance Specialist's is a task in its private folder, found from the role by `private_folder(role)`, and runs there; every rule below keys on that one function, so another role's folder is one more arm of it (6.10). Its readiness is 5.3's `private_folder_task`; its assignment waits while another task holds the folder (5.2), and then Farik copies the folder's files but `.history/` to `.history/<task-id>/`, once, and starts the task there, with no worktree, branch or sandbox made. Its implement session, its reviewer's session and the Product Manager's acceptance session run with the folder as their working directory (made for its owner alone when it is not there) and with no executor, so the hook, which judges every path against the working directory (5.6, 8.2), lets them reach the folder and nothing outside it: not another task's worktree, not `.farik/local/farik.db`, not the files above the folder. The implement message says where the books are and that nothing there is committed. It asks for `verifying` with `farik_request_transition`, whose `workbooks` names the workbooks it wrote, one to twenty paths in the folder (a list of none or of more is `InvalidInput`), and the governor's gate reads them from the folder (5.2); the files that changed since the copy are the task's diff, and the Definition of Done holds them to `allowed_paths` and excepts the folder from the protected paths (5.4). Farik checks its `artifact` criteria on the host and shows its reviewer which files changed, each beside its copy (5.4). Once the Product Manager accepts it, it is finished with nothing to integrate and counts as integrated for the tasks that depend on it (5.14). Each task's copy, `.history/<task-id>/`, is kept, as every version of a workbook under `.history/` is (the tools' own copies, above), and is the restore point for a cancelled task, whose changes stay in the folder. Its prompt and its skill say all this: work in the private folder, nothing there is committed, record each `artifact` criterion with `farik_record_criterion_result` before asking for `verifying`, and ask for it naming the `workbooks`. A session of its ends in one of three ways, as every role's does: it asks the human, declares itself blocked, or asks for `verifying`, and naming the `workbooks` is how the third goes.

Stripe, Digits and Kick (changed in 0.67; phase 7 step 10; ADR 0019's amendment of 2026-10-07). The role reads the services its kit offers (6.7), each connected by the user and each only read. Stripe, when the product uses it, gives the revenue, fees, payouts and refunds, through Stripe's official server: the user signs in to Stripe (route 1), choosing the account, live or a sandbox, and the permissions on Stripe's own page, and can end Farik's access under ‘OAuth sessions’ in their Stripe user settings. Read-only is Farik's own lock, which holds whatever the user ticks on Stripe's page: in the kit the tools that read the business's numbers are `network` and every tool that writes, whatever it writes, is `denied`, so the harness refuses a write before Stripe would. This replaces the restricted key with read permissions that 0.54 described, since a signed-in service refreshes its own access and a kit entry has one way in. Digits and Kick, which a business may already keep its books in, are signed in to and read the same way. The books carry totals and Stripe's own ids, never a customer's name, email or card: `stripe_api_read` takes any `GET`, so a charge comes back with its customer's details, Farik cannot split one tool, and the role's prompt and the skill `using-finance-sources` hold the line. What that leaves open is a known risk (8.6).

Receipts intake (changed in 0.21 on the founder's decision of 2026-09-27, from a connector to the user's main mailbox; ships in the first release after the web release, phase 13 step 02). The user keeps a receipts mailbox of its own, an alias or a second address at their own provider, and forwards or routes receipts to it; vendors can bill it directly. Farik reads that mailbox over IMAP, with an app password, or the provider's IMAP sign-in through Farik Cloud where it suits (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044), kept in the OS keychain; it reads no other mailbox, so the boundary is the mailbox itself rather than a filter Farik promises to apply, and no provider's restricted-scope verification applies. The connector asks for unread messages, has no call that changes the mailbox, never hands over a message already filed, accepts a sender only from the user's approved list, and gives the agent each message's text, and each attachment it files, under the untrusted-content notice (8.6). An attachment is filed only if it is a PDF or an image and at most 10 MB. The mailbox settings, the approved senders and the ledger of filed messages live in `.farik/local/finance/mailbox.json` (8.4). The user drops no bill anywhere; a bank or card statement, the one file every bank exports as CSV, goes under `imports/` and is the source the books are reconciled against, because a receipt describes a purchase and a statement shows the money. `farik finance connect`, `farik finance disconnect` and the web app's finance setup manage the mailbox; `mailbox.connected` and `mailbox.disconnected` record it. A connector to the user's main mailbox through Google's or Microsoft's mail API, filtered by a label or folder, is not planned: it needs Google's restricted-scope verification with a paid yearly assessment, its filter is Farik's promise rather than the provider's enforcement, and it is built only if users ask once receipts intake has shipped.

When it works. While a process drives the project, Farik starts a receipts sweep once a day, as a tick rule like the standup (5.9): the connector fetches the new messages first, and a sweep with nothing new starts no session and costs nothing; otherwise a short Finance Specialist session files each receipt and records it in the books, its cost under the `finance` purpose (F17). `sweep.started` and `sweep.ended` are recorded either way, so the day's tick has a last run to count from. The user can start one with "Check now" or `farik finance sweep`. `receipt.filed` records each receipt. One piece of finance work touches the folder at a time, and a sweep is one: once receipts intake ships, a sweep does not start while a task holds the folder (5.2), and a finance task is not assigned while a sweep runs. A finance task is otherwise a task in the folder, as "Finance tasks, as built" above says: its `allowed_paths` are under `.farik/local/finance/`, its criteria are `artifact`, `review` and `human`, its session runs in the folder, it names its `workbooks` to reach `verifying`, its reviewer is shown what changed beside the copy taken at its assignment, and once `accepted` it is finished (5.14).

### 6.7 Role kits (added in 0.22)

Each role ships a kit, `roles/<role>/kit.yaml`, validated against `kit.schema.json` (ADR 0020; `docs/design/role-kits.md`): the skills it carries, in the Agent Skills format of section 6, and the connectors it may be given, each an MCP server (5.6) with its setup copy for the wizard, the credential keys it needs, every tool tagged by the kit, and its tool list pinned so that a test fails when the service's list drifts, each pin update re-reviewing the tags. Every connector is optional; a role works without any. The user connects one by signing in or pasting a key from the page the copy names, never by tagging a tool, in the web app's connector screens or with `farik connect <agent> <connector>` and `farik disconnect <agent> <connector>`; `connector.connected` and `connector.disconnected` record it (8.5). Credentials are per agent. A kit's connector is connected this way from phase 7 step 05, and signing in from step 03; a server of the user's own is connected now, with pasted keys, as the last paragraph of this section says (added in 0.38). Kits are free (section 9).

A connector's tool is tagged `network`, a read-only remote call that runs; `external_effect`, a call that changes anything outside the sandbox or spends the user's credits, which asks; or `denied`, never offered to the agent. It is never `read`, which means the project's files: a remote read is an outbound call carrying arguments the agent chose. Connecting a connector gives the agent that server's tagged tools whatever the agent's own tiers; the tag governs the call.

A tool that spends the user's credits, generating or editing one image or one video clip, or that only drafts what the human sends from the service itself, such as an email draft in Kit, carries an allowance: the number of calls per sprint, or per UTC day when the project runs no sprints (5.5), that are pre-approved, per tool and per agent, set by the user when connecting from the kit's default. A call beyond the allowance asks the human as any `external_effect` does, and the human may raise it. A batch tool, and any tool that publishes, sends, posts or pays, has no allowance and always asks, unless the owner's approved marketing plan covers the act (6.5; ADR 0042; added in 0.52), as does one a kit judges too costly to pre-approve: a 3D model, a video edit priced by its length, an analysis, or a change to the account's library. A voice-over always asks as well (added in 0.51), because Higgsfield's `generate_audio` can copy a voice from a sample of audio and a kit cannot refuse one argument of a tool, so the human sees the whole input of every one. An image may use an identity the user trained in their own account, which no kit trains, so using it is the user's earlier choice. The count is a projection of the `tool.called` events the hook records for every call (8.5), and the board shows it against the allowance beside the model spend. An allowance counts calls, not money, because Farik cannot price another service's credits. Everything a connector returns is untrusted content (8.6), and a message read from a chat bridge answers no question and approves nothing.

Allowances in the team file (added in 0.45; ADR 0037). A kit entry in `team.yaml` may hold `allowances: { <tool>: 0..1000 }`, per agent: `allowance_not_kit` on an entry that is not `source: kit`, `allowance_not_external` for a tool it does not tag `external_effect`, each at `/agents/<i>/mcp_servers/<j>/allowances/<tool>`. `spec_sha256` holds `allowances` only when it is not empty, so a commit that raises one makes the server "Connect again". At connect each starts at the kit's `calls`; `connector.connect` and `farik connect --allowance <tool>=<n>` (kit connectors only, `kit_names_these` otherwise) set them, `allowance_not_offered` refuses a tool the kit gives none, `allowance_out_of_range` a number outside 0 to 1,000. `connector.allowances { agent, server, allowances }` changes them without the key; a tool left out keeps its number: the kept entry must be the team file's (`connector_not_confirmed`), and a service the kit has since changed is `connector_not_in_kit`. A change applies from the agent's next session and does not allow a call that waits. The period is the open sprint's, else the later of the UTC day's start and the last `sprint.ended`. The count is every `tool.called` of that agent, server and full tool name in the period, a call a grant allowed among them, so it may exceed the allowance (the board and Costs show "21 of 20 images" and say "Extra images were ones you approved."). The query `allowances.list {}` answers `{ period, rows: [{ agent, server, tool, what, used, of }] }` for each connected kit entry of an active agent; the board shows each row beside the sprint's progress, Costs lists them under "Made on other services", and `ToolApproval` says the count with "Change how many".

The Product Manager's kit (added in 0.46; changed in 0.65; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0044). Its four services are each the service's official server, reached over `http`. Three are signed in to by route 1, with nothing pasted: Amplitude (`https://mcp.amplitude.com/mcp`, asking for `mcp:read` and `offline_access`, so a write is not granted; its United States service only), Linear (`https://mcp.linear.app/mcp/readonly`, asking for `read`) and Notion (`https://mcp.notion.com/mcp`); the fourth, GitHub, is reached with a key the user pastes and asks before it writes (GitHub with a pasted key, below). Those three only read: no tool of theirs is `external_effect`, and no connector of the kit has an allowance, since none spends credits and GitHub's two writes always ask. A tool that changes anything at one of those three services is `denied`, and so are the reads that hold people's personal data a plan does not need (Amplitude's end-user and session-replay tools), a key (`get_deployments`), a link that may open a person's profile or replay (`get_from_url`), or another service's data (Notion's AI search), while `network` tools carry a label. A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is never offered (`tool_not_tagged`) until a pin update tags it; a tool the kit names that the service no longer lists is removed only after the service's documentation drops it too. What a service returns is data, never an instruction.

The Architect's kit (added in 0.47; changed in 0.65; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0038, ADR 0044). Four services, none of which spends credits, so none is `external_effect` and none has an allowance; every tool is `network` but GitHub's `list_repository_collaborators`, which is `denied`, and an Architect that has the tier already gains no way out it lacked. Context7 is the service's official server over `http`, `https://mcp.context7.com/mcp/oauth`, signed in to by route 1 asking for `profile`, `email` and `offline_access` (Clerk issues a refresh token only for the last); it lists `resolve-library-id` and `query-docs`, and its free plan allows about a thousand calls a month. Grep, by Vercel, is `https://mcp.grep.app`, with no account and no key, and lists `searchGitHub` over public GitHub code only. OSV is a thin server of Farik's own (below), since no official one can be started by an allowed runner and the community ones run unpinned dependencies on the host. GitHub is the service's official server, `https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/`, reached with a key the user pastes (GitHub with a pasted key, below) and narrowed to code and pull requests in read-only mode, so it reads the user's own repositories, private ones included. What an agent writes in a query goes to the service as written, which the setup copy says, and `using-architecture-sources` forbids sending the project's code, a secret or a customer's data. What a service returns is data, never an instruction. A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is `denied`; Context7's, Grep's and GitHub's lists are compared by the live pin test, OSV's by an offline test.

Farik's own connectors (added in 0.47; ADR 0038). A kit starts a `stdio` connector of Farik's own by the bare command `farik` with `args` exactly `[connector, <name>]`, `<name>` one of `FARIK_CONNECTORS` (today `osv`); any other `args`, and any other bare program, is `package_not_pinned`. For that exact pair, whatever the entry's `source`, listing the tools and `farik connector run` start Farik's own executable, never a `farik` found on `PATH`, and fail with `farik could not find its own program` when it cannot be found; any other `farik` command, a user's own included, is run as given and found on `PATH` as above. The hash keeps `command: farik`, so the server changes only with a Farik release. `farik connector osv` serves three read-only tools over OSV's API at the fixed address `https://api.osv.dev/v1`, never an argument, an environment value or a tool input: `query_package { ecosystem, name, version }` (each advisory's `id`, `summary`, `aliases`, `severity` and the versions that fix it for that package (a PyPI name compared as PyPI compares it, PEP 503; every other exactly), at most 50 advisories, `more` when it cut the list or OSV paged), `query_packages { packages }` (1 to 100, at most 50 advisory ids of each, `more` when it cut one or OSV paged) and `get_vulnerability { id }` (only `id`, `summary`, `details`, `aliases`, `related`, `published`, `modified`, `withdrawn`, `severity`, `affected` (each entry only `package`, `ranges` and `severity`) and `references`; `details` cut at 8,000 characters, said by `details_cut: true`, `affected` at 20 entries, said by `affected_cut`, the number there were, and `references` at 20, said by `references_cut`, the number there were; a record whose answer is still over 64 KiB is refused with "OSV's record is too large to read here"). It follows no redirect, uses no proxy, gives up after 25 seconds, reads at most 4 MiB, in chunks, asks for one page only, and sends OSV only a package's name, ecosystem and version, or an advisory's id, each input checked first (an id is one percent-encoded path segment). It runs with the connector's cleared environment, so a user behind a proxy gets a tool error.

The built-in connector (added in 0.33; ADR 0026). Before any kit, Farik ships one connector of its own, Playwright (5.6), held to the same rules: the `container` connector `playwright` of the UI/UX Designer's kit, `roles/ui_ux_designer/kit.yaml` (changed in 0.44, from `crates/roles/connectors/playwright.yaml`), pins its image by digest (`@playwright/mcp` 0.0.82 on 2026-09-30), names the folder its Playwright is kept in, and tags every tool the image lists, the browsing tools `network` and every other `denied`. A test in Docker lists the pinned image's tools against the kit, and a tool a new pin adds goes in as `denied` in the same change, until a plan tags it ("add each as denied in roles/ui_ux_designer/kit.yaml"). `builtin_connector(name)` answers what the kit holds; the team file still names it `{ name: playwright, source: builtin }`.

The Marketing Specialist's kit (added in 0.51; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0037, ADR 0040). Two creative services, each the service's official server over `http` and signed in to by route 1, with nothing pasted, and each spending the user's credits; the posting and email services follow in the next paragraph. Higgsfield (`https://mcp.higgsfield.ai/mcp`, asking for `openid`, `email` and `offline_access`) needs a paid plan, and every generation through an agent uses credits whatever the plan, which its setup copy says. Recraft (`https://mcp.recraft.ai/mcp`, asking for `openid`, `email` and `profile`) has no `offline_access` scope and no revocation address, so its grant may lapse when it expires and the agent then shows "Sign in again", which its setup copy says too. A tool that makes one image, clip or edit is `external_effect` with an allowance: Higgsfield's `generate_image` 20 "images", `generate_video` 6 "video requests", `upscale_image` 10 "image upscales", `remove_background` 10 "background removals" and `outpaint_image` 10 "image extensions"; Recraft's `generate_image` 20 "images", `image_to_image` 10 "image edits", `vectorize_image` 10 "vector conversions", `remove_background` 10 "background removals", `replace_background` 10 "background swaps" and `crisp_upscale` 10 "image upscales". An allowance counts calls, not credits, and one Higgsfield call may make up to four pictures (`count`) and one Recraft call up to six (`numberOfImages`), so `making-images-and-video` has the agent ask for one in each call. A price check, `generate_video` with `get_cost` true, is a call that counts against `generate_video`'s allowance, which is why that allowance counts video requests and the skill allows one check per brief. Every other tool that spends credits always asks: Higgsfield's voice-over (above), its batches, presets, ad sets, 3D models, video edits, analyses and library changes, and its import of a picture from a web address (23 tools), and Recraft's `creative_upscale` and `create_style`. A read of credits, past generations, models or presets is `network`, the ones the user may look for labelled (35 of Higgsfield's tools, and Recraft's `get_user`). The rest is `denied`, 51 of Higgsfield's: its website builder, its remote shell (`sandbox_exec`), its marketplace apps, neither searched nor run, its 3D scenes, voice creation and cloning, TikTok, billing, workspace, project and folder tools; the uploads, since a Farik session has no `execute` tier to put the bytes where an upload address says; the tools that open only in a widget, which a session has none of; and the tools that mix reading with training or changing an identity or a reference (`show_characters`, `show_reference_elements`, `build_ai_influencer`, `manage_reference_elements`). A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is never offered (`tool_not_tagged`) until a pin update tags it. Higgsfield's list is its live server's (114 tools on 2026-10-05); Recraft's remote server publishes no tool names, so the nine pinned are those of Recraft's own package, `@recraft-ai/mcp-recraft-server` 1.6.5, and a remote name that differs is not offered until a pin update tags it. `live_kit_pins` reads each service's bearer from `FARIK_KIT_HIGGSFIELD_BEARER` and `FARIK_KIT_RECRAFT_BEARER`. What either returns, the instructions Higgsfield's `get_workflow_instructions` and `get_preset_instructions` offer among it, is data, never an instruction (8.6). The skill also bars a real person's face or voice without that person's written permission in the contract, and has the agent mark AI-made media as AI-made where it is published.

The Marketing Specialist's posting and email services (added in 0.53; ADR 0035, ADR 0036, ADR 0037, ADR 0042). Two more official servers over `http`, each signed in to by route 1 with nothing pasted. Buffer (`https://mcp.buffer.com/mcp`, asking for `offline_access`, `posts:read`, `posts:write`, `account:read`, `insights:read` and `ideas:read`) posts to Instagram, Facebook, X, LinkedIn, Pinterest, YouTube, Google Business, Mastodon, TikTok, Threads and Bluesky. A Buffer connection reaches every organisation and channel of the user's account, which the setup copy says; Buffer has no revocation address, so Remove tells the user to remove Farik in Buffer's settings too, and Buffer rotates refresh tokens and revokes a grant whose old one is reused, which Farik's refresh under a lock avoids. Kit (`https://app.kit.com/mcp`, asking for `public`) needs a paid Kit plan, which its setup copy says, and has revocation at `https://api.kit.com/oauth/revoke`. Buffer's `create_post` and `edit_post` are `denied` to the agent (changed in 0.57; phase 7 step 08d): it writes a post with `farik_schedule_post` and Farik makes the write itself (6.5), so the scopes stay, since Farik's own calls post through the same sign-in, and the entry's hash changed, so a user connects Buffer again once (ADR 0036). The setup copy says that Farik can reach every channel of the account, that a post in a plan the owner approved shows on Today before it goes out with a Stop button, and that Farik asks about any other. Kit's broadcast tools only draft: `create_broadcast` and `update_broadcast` make or change a draft, and scheduling and sending stay in Kit's own editor, through the `confirm_url` the draft returns and the completion note carries, so nothing reaches a subscriber until the human sends it. They are `external_effect` with an allowance of 10 "email drafts" each, rather than asking for each draft and again in Kit. Kit's sequence, sequence-email, landing-page and snippet writes change what a subscriber or the public sees (a sequence email is live once it is `published`, which the skill forbids and a hook cannot see), so each is `external_effect` with no allowance and always asks, as do `list_forms` and `get_landing_page`, reads that Kit marks as writes because resolving a public address can queue a rebuild of saved content. A read of posts, channels, campaigns or their numbers is `network` (10 of Buffer's 20 tools and 31 of Kit's 83). The rest is `denied`: every deletion, since a post or an email cannot be taken back; Buffer's idea and template writes and its generic `introspect_schema`, `execute_query` and `execute_mutation`, which reach the whole API and so cannot be tagged, and, since 0.57, `create_post` and `edit_post` (10 of Buffer's); and, in Kit (40 of its 83), every write to subscribers, tags, custom fields, products, colours or webhooks, `unsubscribe` among them, every bulk tool, `list_tax_codes` and `list_webhooks`, and every read of a subscriber's or a buyer's own data, for the reason the Product Manager's kit denies Amplitude's end-user tools: a campaign needs totals, not people. A tool a service lists that the kit does not name is never offered (`tool_not_tagged`) until a pin update tags it. Buffer's twenty tools are those of its published guide to its server and Kit's 83 those of its help centre's list of its connector's tools (both read 2026-10-05); `live_kit_pins` reads their bearers from `FARIK_KIT_BUFFER_BEARER` and `FARIK_KIT_KIT_BEARER`. The role's words change with them: its prompt, its `forbidden` list and its skills no longer say it never publishes. Since step 08c (changed in 0.55) its `forbidden` list says it publishes, sends or spends money except through a call the owner allows or the owner's approved marketing plan (6.5), and since step 08d (changed in 0.57) its skills say that a social post in the owner's approved marketing plan goes out through `farik_schedule_post`, that any other post waits for the owner, that an email is a draft the human sends, and that otherwise the human publishes; the plan's ads come with step 08f. The ninth skill, `posting-and-email`, is the email skill since 0.57 (its posting rules went to `running-social-channels`, the kit's fourteenth skill, which has the agent read `list_channels`, fill the active plan's slots with `farik_schedule_post` at least three hours ahead and on the slot's day in the business's own offset, with `details` for YouTube and Pinterest, use only the business's own pictures, ones the owner gave it or ones it made, and read results with `get_aggregated_post_metrics` before the next plan). It has the agent hand over a broadcast's `confirm_url`, keep a series' emails unpublished, keep each email or page under the 64 KiB input limit, a longer one becoming two emails in a series or a shorter page, since Kit's updates replace the whole body (`tool_input_too_large`), and never put a subscriber's details in a prompt or a note. Search-engine data (Semrush) is not in the kit: it publishes no tool names and its access goes in the request body, which Farik's header cannot set; per-network servers are not in it either, since X's own server cannot post and charges per post, Meta admits only listed clients, and LinkedIn, TikTok and YouTube have none.

The Finance Specialist's kit (added in 0.67; ADR 0019's amendment of 2026-10-07, ADR 0035, ADR 0036). Six skills, `categorising-expenses`, `closing-the-month`, `forecasting`, `unit-economics-and-pricing`, `recommending-a-budget` and `using-finance-sources`, and three services, each the service's official server over `http` and signed in to by route 1, with nothing pasted. Stripe (`https://mcp.stripe.com`) asks for the one scope `mcp`, pinned because with none named Farik would ask for every scope the server advertises, a wider one added later among them; Digits (`https://api.digits.com/mcp`) names none; Kick (`https://use.kick.co/mcp`) asks for `mcp:read` alone, which Kick documents as the scope for any call that only looks up data, so a write is refused at Kick as well as here. Digits and Kick are bookkeeping services a business may already keep its books in, which the role reads in place of rebuilding them, and are not Kit, the Marketing Specialist's email service; each is the business's own account, and Farik adds nothing to its cost. The role only reads: seven of Stripe's ten tools, nine of Digits' ten and seventeen of Kick's 39 are `network`, each with a label, and the rest are `denied`, which is every tool that writes whatever it writes (`stripe_api_write`, Kick's `*_act`, `*_create` and `*_update` tools and `activity_undo`), `send_stripe_feedback`, which sends something to Stripe's company, `stripe_implementation_planner`, which builds an integration and reads no numbers, `list_business_users`, the people with access to the books, which the role does not need, and Kick's `list_kick_skills` and `load_kick_skill`, which load Kick's own instructions into the session, where the role's instructions are Farik's skills and a service's words are data (8.6). No tool is `external_effect`, so no service has an allowance. A service lists the tools its grant allows (Kick: “Available Kick tools can vary by token scope”), so a pinned tool a grant does not list costs nothing (ADR 0036), and the live pin test lists each service with a bearer from a sign-in granted widely, which the kit never asks for. The skills' rules: a category is one line of the books' `Categories` sheet, and a new one has a reason in its row's note, with no tax category given as advice; a month is closed only when every difference between Stripe, the ledger, Farik's costs and the books of more than one per cent is explained in a row's note with both figures, by a `Status` cell on the month's `Monthly summary` row reading `closed`; a forecast has a method named, a range, its assumptions on a sheet of their own, and its date and inputs; a price change and a budget are recommendations the user decides, a daily limit in Settings and a sprint's when the user starts it (5.5); and every figure from a service names its source and its dates. Stripe's tools are those of its documentation, Digits' of its help page for its server and Kick's 39 of its tool reference (each read 2026-10-07); `live_kit_pins` reads their bearers from `FARIK_KIT_STRIPE_BEARER`, `FARIK_KIT_DIGITS_BEARER` and `FARIK_KIT_KICK_BEARER`. QuickBooks Online is not in the kit: whether its registration accepts Farik is for a later step to probe, and Xero's server registers no client by itself, so it waits for Farik Cloud.

Farik calls a connector's tool itself (added in 0.57; phase 7 step 08d, ADR 0042's amendment). `connectors::call_tool` makes one call of one tool of a server, beside `list_tools`, with the same start (a `stdio` server in its folder with only its keys and a few safe variables, an `http` one with its filled headers and bearer): one MCP session for the call, opened, `tools/call`, cancelled, within 30 seconds, past which it is `ConnectorError::Timeout`. It answers the result's structured content when there is one, else its first text block read as JSON, else `{ "text": <text> }`; a result marked as an error, and a JSON-RPC error to the call, are `ConnectorError::ToolError` with the text cut at 500 characters, which Farik passes on only as untrusted words. Only a fixed list of Farik's own calls is ever made, `OWN_CALLS` (`daemon/own_calls.rs`): Buffer's `get_channel`, `create_post` and `delete_post`; any other pair is refused before anything starts, and a later step that has Farik call a service itself adds its pairs to the list and calls through `call_as`, never beside it. `call_as(state, agent, server, tool, arguments)` takes the named agent's kit entry from the team file, requires that the entry still matches its role's kit and that the connection is kept (`NotConnected` otherwise), runs it in the connector folder with Farik's own program, and refreshes a signed-in entry first as a session's start does, within 60 seconds of its expiring, falling back to a token still good (a lapse is `SignInAgain`); an entry with keys is used as kept. Its errors are `NotListed`, `NotConnected`, `SignInAgain`, `Failed`, `Timeout` and `Tool`. A call of Farik's is not the agent's: it passes no hook, may name a tool the kit tags `denied` (Buffer's `delete_post`), and is recorded as the `social_post.*` events (6.5), so it charges none of the agent's tool-call limit for an act that is the owner's; it uses the connection of the agent whose post it is, and a post of an agent whose Buffer entry is gone, lapsed or changed fails at hand-over, saying so.

A user's own connectors (added in 0.38; ADR 0030). Before any kit, the user can give one agent any MCP server: a program Farik starts on this computer (`stdio`) or a web address spoken to over streamable HTTP (`http`; the older SSE transport is not accepted). Its entry in the agent's `mcp_servers` is `{ name, source: custom, transport, command, args, url, headers, credential_keys, tools }`, checked by `validate_team`, each refusal at its own field: `transport` is required with `source: custom` and absent with `builtin`, which takes none of these fields; `command` and `args` go with `stdio` alone and `command` is required there, a bare name found on `PATH` or a full path, so a `command` holding a `/` that does not start with one, such as `./server` or `bin/mcp`, is refused (`command_not_absolute`), `url` and `headers` with `http` alone and `url` is required there; `farik` and a built-in's name are `connector_name_reserved`; a `url` starts `http://` or `https://` and holds no userinfo and no query (`url_holds_secret`), because it is written to the committed team file as it is, so a service whose address holds its key cannot be added yet; `headers` (at most 16) are templates that may hold `{KEY}` for a key in `credential_keys` and nothing else (`header_key_unknown`), and a header with a word, split at `-` and `_` in any case, of `authorization`, `cookie`, `key`, `apikey`, `token`, `secret`, `auth`, `password`, `passwd`, `pass`, `credential` or `credentials` in its name must hold one and no value beside it: split at spaces and `;`, each piece is a `{KEY}`, a `name={KEY}`, or, first, a scheme of a letter then letters and digits such as `Bearer` or `OAuth2` (`header_holds_secret`; beside a placeholder, and `Cookie`, added in 0.38 after review), so no secret is in the file; an argument is refused at its place (`arg_holds_secret`, added in 0.38 after review) when it is the value given to a flag that names a key, as `--api-key abc`, `--token=abc` or `--api-key -abc`, the value not a number and not a flag starting `--`; when it is `NAME=value` with a name that names a key, as `API_KEY=abc`; when it is a credential header with its value, as `mcp-remote` takes after `--header`; or when it is shaped like a well-known service's key (`sk-`, `sk_live_`, `sk_test_`, `rk_live_`, `rk_test_`, `ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghs_`, `github_pat_`, `glpat-`, `xoxb-`, `xoxp-`, or `AKIA` and 16 capitals and digits). A name names a key when its last word at `-` and `_` is `token`, `password`, `passwd`, `pass`, `secret`, `auth`, `pat`, `apikey`, `credential` or `credentials`, or its last two are `api key`, `access key` or `secret key`, and its first is not `no`: `--key`, `--sort-key`, `--primary-key`, `--max-token 4096` and `--no-auth` are settings, its value never quoted, since a server's key goes in `credential_keys` and reaches it in its environment; `credential_keys` are at most 8 names matching `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{0,63}$`; and `tools` maps each tool to `network`, `external_effect` or `denied`. Connecting lists the server's tools: Farik starts it, or reaches it, with the agent's keys, through the launcher's clean environment (8.2), and gives up after thirty seconds. A tool whose name falls outside `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`, which Claude Code would rewrite, is shown as one Farik can't use and is not written, so a call to it is `tool_not_tagged`. The user labels each other tool; one left unlabelled is written `external_effect`. The tools written at connect are the server's pin: a tool it lists later is `tool_not_tagged` until the user connects it again. The agent's keys are its own, never another agent's: Farik keeps them in the OS keychain, service `farik`, account `connector:<project_id>:<agent_id>:<server>`, where `<project_id>` is the project's id on this machine: 32 random hex digits made the first time it is needed and kept in the user's Farik state folder at `projects/<sha256 of the project's canonical path>` (the folder 0700, the file 0600, refused unless it holds 32 hex digits), never in the project, and not the log's `project_id`, which is the folder's name, so two projects in folders of one name, a clone into a folder of the same name, or a copy of the project's folder never find each other's keys (changed in 0.38 after review). A project moved to another path is a new project here, and its connectors are connected again. Keys are kept as one JSON object `{ spec_sha256, keys }` holding only the keys the entry names, and a server with no keys still gets one. On a computer with no keychain they go to `connectors.json` in the user's Farik state folder under the same account, the folder 0700 and the file 0600, written beside and renamed over while a lock file, `connectors.json.lock`, holds off any other process; Farik reads both stores, so an entry kept in the file before a keychain appeared is still found. With neither, connecting is refused `no_secret_store`. `spec_sha256` is the sha256 of the canonical JSON (compact, object keys sorted at every depth) of the entry's `transport`, `command`, `args`, `url`, `headers`, `credential_keys` and `tools`; the name is not in it, being part of where the keys are kept. What was connected on this machine is what runs: an entry the team file changed since, by a clone, a pulled branch, a template or a hand edit, or one never connected here, is unconfirmed, and runs nothing and is sent no key until the user connects it again (8.2), so a commit can neither move a server's address nor relabel a `denied` tool `network`. A `stdio` server runs on the host as the user, with the user's rights, in sandbox mode too, and always in a folder Farik keeps for it outside the repository, `connectors/<project id>/<agent>/<server>` in the user's Farik state folder, removed and made again, empty and 0700, each time its tools are listed and each time a session starts it (changed in 0.38 after review). When that state folder is inside the project, as when `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` points into it, no stdio server is listed or started, and the refusal `state_inside_project` says so on the page and at the command line (added in 0.38 after review). Connecting a server again while a session runs it removes and remakes the folder it runs in, so that server's writes into its folder fail until it next starts; this fails closed, and Farik runs one session at a time. Never in a task's worktree, which agents write to, nor anywhere in the repository: git checks out a file committed there on a clone or a pull, and `npx`, `uvx` and `uv run` look upward to the repository's root, so a planted `node_modules/.bin`, `.npmrc`, module, `pyproject.toml` or script would run on the host with the agent's keys. With no state folder (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `HOME` and `APPDATA` all unset), no stdio server is listed or started. Outside the repository is all the folder promises: a command on `PATH` that itself reads a project, say one given the repository's path as an argument, is the user's own choice. A relative path in `args` resolves in that folder, so a script of the user's is given by its full path. The program is the one the user named: a bare name is found on `PATH`. On the command line (unix), `farik connect <agent> <name> (--command <program> [--arg <a>]... | --url <url> [--header '<Name>: <template>']...) [--key <NAME>]... [--tag <tool>=network|external_effect|denied]...` checks the entry against the team, reads each key's value from a line of standard input, without echo at a terminal, never from an argument, and refuses `--key NAME=value` without printing the value. It lists the tools in its own process, prints each with its tag, or that Farik can't use it, labels a tool no `--tag` names `external_effect`, refuses a `--tag` for a tool the server did not list or one Farik can't use (`tag_unknown_tool`, naming the tools it lists), so a misspelling never leaves the tool meant `external_effect` unsaid, and keeps the keys in its own process, so a key never crosses a socket. It then writes the team file and records `connector.connected` here, holding the run lock, when nothing drives the project, or else sends the command `connector_connect { agent, server, spec_sha256 }`, `server` the entry with no value in it; and it ends with "Kept in your computer's keychain" or "Kept in a private file only you can read". `--json` prints `{ tools, stored_in, said }`. `connector_connect` is refused when the entry breaks the team's rules, is not a custom server (`connector_not_custom`), or does not hash to `spec_sha256` (`connector_not_confirmed`). `farik disconnect <agent> <name>` sends `connector_disconnect { agent, server }` first, which removes the entry and records `connector.disconnected` and is not found unless the agent has a custom server of that name, so a refused one deletes nothing; it then deletes that agent's keys from both stores and touches no other agent's. Keys are kept before the command runs, so a command refused after its check leaves keys with no entry, which run nothing and are replaced by the next connect. Retiring an agent, or removing it from the team by a save or a saved team applied, deletes its keys for each of its custom servers, and no other agent's; a save that takes one custom server from an agent deletes that agent's keys for it (added in 0.38 after review). A server that fails to answer, or to list its tools, is said to have failed in Farik's words alone: its own error text, which may quote what it was sent, is not repeated. The web app does the same through three RPCs (F9).
Signing in to a service (added in 0.41; ADR 0033). An `http` server, or Farik's own connector (a `stdio` entry that is exactly `farik connector <name>`, ADR 0038; amended in 0.59), may be signed in to instead of given keys: its entry then holds `oauth: { client_id?, callback_port?, scopes? }`, and the presence of `oauth` means "signed in". `client_id` is a public client the service's app registration gave Farik, at most 200 characters; `callback_port`, from 1024 to 65535, goes with `client_id` alone (`callback_port_without_client`); `scopes` are at most 16, each of RFC 6749's scope characters, so none holds a space or a quote. `validate_team` refuses `oauth` on a `stdio` server (`oauth_on_stdio`) unless it is exactly Farik's own connector's pair, the command `farik` and the arguments `connector` and a word matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$` (the kit loader holds the word to Farik's own connectors), on which `client_id` and `callback_port` are refused (`farik_connector_client`: the app's id and port are Farik's); it refuses `oauth` beside `credential_keys` (`oauth_with_keys`) and beside a header named `Authorization` in any case (`oauth_header_conflict`), each at its field. `spec_sha256` holds `"oauth": { client_id, callback_port, scopes }` only when `oauth` is present, so every entry connected before keeps its hash, and a change to one of the three makes the server "Connect again". Farik runs the sign-in, in the daemon for the web app and in `farik connect`'s own process for the command line, through `rmcp`'s OAuth client with Farik's own checks around it, because Claude Code's `-p` cannot sign in and keeps its tokens by its config folder, not by agent. It asks the server to initialize without a token and follows the `resource_metadata` of its 401's `WWW-Authenticate` (RFC 9728) and nothing else: a server that does not point to its metadata is `sign_in_not_offered`, and no endpoint is guessed. The authorization server must say it does PKCE with `S256` (`pkce_not_supported` otherwise, before anything is registered), name an `issuer`, and offer either a `registration_endpoint` or have the team file's `client_id` (`sign_in_not_supported` otherwise); every URL it names, the server's own, and every redirect Farik is sent along must be `https`, or `http` on this computer's loopback, which the tests need, or the sign-in fails naming it. Registration, in order: the `client_id` of the entry, with no registration request, else dynamic client registration (`client_name` `Farik`, `application_type` `native`, `token_endpoint_auth_method` `none`, this attempt's redirect), once per sign-in, else `sign_in_not_supported`. Farik takes no client secret from a team file or from a user: it is a public client. No build uses a secret of Farik's: from the web launch Farik Cloud adds one where a service needs it (below; changed in 0.61, ADR 0044). The redirect is `http://localhost:<port>/callback`, `localhost` because authorization servers match a registered address exactly; the listener binds `127.0.0.1` and, where there is IPv6, `[::1]`, never every address, on a free port tried up to five times, or on `callback_port` (33418 when none is given) for a given client, which fails `sign_in_failed` naming the port when it is taken. It answers the first `GET /callback` whose `state` is the attempt's, a random single-use value, with a plain page, the Farik mark, a headline and a sentence, that says how it went from Farik's own reason and quotes nothing the service sent, no link and no script, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; img-src data:`, `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, and closes; another path is 404 and another `state` 400, neither ending the attempt. An attempt lasts ten minutes (`sign_in_timed_out`), and a new one for the same agent and server ends the old. So does the page's Cancel: `connector.sign_in_cancel { attempt }` stops the attempt's polling or its listener, waits for that, and drops the attempt with whatever the service gave it, a grant approved already included, so a sign-in the person says yes to after Cancel is never kept; an attempt already gone or used up is not a refusal, and the answer is `{}`. `iss` (RFC 9207), when the callback carries one, must equal the metadata's `issuer`, and must be there when the metadata says `authorization_response_iss_parameter_supported`, else `sign_in_mismatch`, an error callback with another `iss` included, whose `error` is then not shown; `error=access_denied` is `access_denied`. `resource` (RFC 8707) is the protected-resource metadata's, which must be the server's address or a path above it, and is sent on the authorization, token and refresh requests. The grant, `{ issuer, resource, client_id, token_endpoint, revocation_endpoint?, access_token, refresh_token?, issued_at, expires_at?, scopes, lapsed }`, is kept in the agent's entry, in the keychain or `connectors.json` beside any keys, under the same account, and never prints. Refresh and revocation are Farik's own form requests to the grant's endpoints, through the same check: a session setup refreshes a token that would expire within the session's wall clock and five minutes, the launch route one that would within a minute, saving a rotated refresh token before the new access token is used, under one lock per entry that connect, disconnect and the deletes of removing an agent or a server also take, so a token refreshed while the entry is removed is not kept. A refresh the service refuses with `invalid_grant`, `invalid_client` or `unauthorized_client` lapses the grant: the server is left out of sessions and `team.get` says `sign_in_again`; any other failure uses a token that is still good, and leaves the server out of that session alone when it is not. Remove deletes the entry and then asks the service, within five seconds and without failing the removal, to forget the grant (RFC 7009), the refresh token first; connecting again over another grant asks it to forget the one replaced, unless that is the grant just kept or its client's. A refreshed grant is not saved over an entry that was deleted or changed outside the daemon's lock, and the token just rotated is revoked instead. The page says "Signed in to <host>" for a signed-in row; a lapsed row says "<host> ended Farik's sign-in. Sign in again to use it." with "Sign in again", which opens `ConnectorAdd` filled in from the team file at the sign-in; Remove says Farik deletes the sign-in and asks the service to forget it, or, where the service has no revocation endpoint, that to remove Farik completely the user also removes it in the service's settings. On `ConnectorAdd`, Next for a web address with no key typed asks the service first: where it offers signing in the page shows "Sign in with <issuer host>", with "for <address host>" beneath it when the two differ, opens the page in a new tab in the click itself, and checks every two seconds, then "Signed in to <host>" and on to labelling; "Use a key instead" shows the key fields, and a service that does not offer, or cannot be signed in to, shows them, with a sentence for the last two. On the command line, `farik connect <agent> <name> --url <url> --sign-in [--client-id <id> [--callback-port <port>]] [--scope <s>]... [--tag <tool>=...]...` conflicts with `--key` and `--command`, writes `Sign in to <host> in your browser: <url>` to the error stream, opens it with the system's opener (an opener that fails is no error), waits ten minutes, writes `Signed in to <issuer>.` there, then prints the tools and where the sign-in is kept; `farik disconnect` asks the service to forget a sign-in it held. The loopback redirect assumes the browser and the daemon share a computer, which the daemon's serving `127.0.0.1` alone guarantees (8.6).

Farik's own apps (added in 0.58; ADR 0035, route 2; phase 7 step 03b). A service that registers no client by itself but takes a public one is signed in to with an app Farik registered with it, so that the user makes none. `farik_runtime::registered_apps::REGISTERED_APPS` is a static table with one `RegisteredApp` per app: its `id`, its `name` (what the screens call it), the one `host` it serves (none for an app that signs in for one of Farik's own connectors, which serves no address), the `farik_connector` it signs in for (if any), its `flow`, its public `client_id`, the `client_secret` a service insists on for a client it calls public (if any), the `scopes` it asks for, its `issuer`, its `token_endpoint`, a `revocation_endpoint` when it has one, an `install_url` when the app must be installed on what an agent is to read, and a `settings_url`, the page where the user removes the app at the service. It ships with no entry (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044): Farik's GitHub App (host `api.githubcopilot.com`) and Farik's Google app are registered in phase 11 and held by Farik Cloud, never in a build, so no sign-in runs on the table yet, and for GitHub's address `ConnectorAdd` falls to the key fields, where a fine-grained token is pasted; the tests pass tables of their own, which the daemon takes from `DaemonState::set_registered_apps` and the command line from `CliIo`. An app is matched by the server's address alone, before any discovery, and never by what the server's metadata says, since a server could name another authorization server and receive a token from Farik's app: the address is parsed as a URL, its scheme is `https` on port 443 (or `http` on a loopback host, which the tests need), it holds no user name or password, and its host equals the app's, ignoring ASCII case, with no trailing dot. A `client_id` of an app's in the team file is used only for the address that app serves: for any other it is refused `sign_in_failed` ("this sign-in is only for <name>'s own servers") before any request; on that address, with no `client_id` of the user's own or with the app's, the app signs in and the team file's `scopes` are ignored; any other `client_id` runs the sign-in above unchanged. GitHub's flow is the device flow (RFC 8628): Farik posts the `client_id` with no `scope`, since a GitHub App's permissions are set on the app, to the device endpoint; the answer's `verification_uri` must be the app's own page, else `sign_in_failed` before anything is polled; the user types the `user_code` on that page and says yes; and Farik polls the token endpoint from inside `SignIn::finish`, so that dropping the attempt, as the daemon does to an old attempt for a new one, stops the polling, first after the answer's `interval` (5 seconds when it gives none, never under 1) and then at every interval, adding 5 seconds to every later wait for each `slow_down`. `authorization_pending` waits, `access_denied` is `access_denied`, `expired_token` and ten minutes from the start are `sign_in_timed_out`, and any other `error` is `sign_in_failed`. A poll whose answer is not the service's, a connection that fails, a reply that is not a JSON object (a gateway's 502 page) or a server error with no `error` code in it, does not end the sign-in: Farik waits the interval and polls again, and ends it `sign_in_failed` after three such answers in a row, an address refused as not `https` ending it at once. Every request to the device and token endpoints, a refresh included, sends `Accept: application/json`, since GitHub otherwise answers form-encoded, and an `error` in an answer is read before its status, since GitHub answers one with 200. The device code never leaves Farik, and no listener, `state` or PKCE is involved. The grant is step 03's with `app` (the app's `id`; a grant kept before this has none), an empty `scopes`, the app's issuer and token endpoint, the server's address as `resource` and no revocation endpoint, since revoking needs the client secret, which is never shipped or sent; so Remove cannot ask GitHub to forget it and says where to remove Farik. A refresh of a grant with an `app` sends no `resource`, and `bad_refresh_token` and `incorrect_client_credentials` lapse any grant, as `invalid_grant` does. GitHub keeps at most 10 tokens for a user, an app and a scope and ends the oldest, so a grant replaced by "Connect again" more than ten times can lapse an older agent's, which then shows "Sign in again". `connector.sign_in` answers `{ attempt, authorize_url, issuer, provider?, user_code?, install_url? }`, the last three only when an app signs in, `authorize_url` then being its `verification_uri`; `team.get`'s connector rows say `provider?` and `settings_url?` for a kept grant whose app the daemon's table has. On `ConnectorAdd`, where an app signs in, the page says "<Provider> lets you sign in." with "Sign in with <Provider>", no "for <host>" line, and "Farik shows you a short code to type on <Provider>'s page."; the button shows the code in a card, "Enter this code on <Provider>:", the code large in the code face, "Only enter a code that this page shows you. Farik never sends you a code in a chat.", "Copy the code", "Open <page>" (the page's host and path, `github.com/login/device`), which opens it in a new tab in the click itself, and "Waiting for you on <Provider>…", checking every two seconds for up to ten minutes; once the user has said yes, "Signed in to <Provider>." and, where the app has an `install_url`, "To let <Name> read private repositories, install Farik on them on <Provider>." with the link "Install Farik on <Provider>" in a new tab. An agent's row says "Signed in to <Provider>" in place of the address's host, a kit service's "Signed in to <Provider>." in place of its title, and Remove, where the service cannot be asked to forget the sign-in, says "To remove Farik completely, also remove it in <Provider>'s settings." with the name a link to `settings_url` in a new tab (naming the connector for one with neither a provider nor a web address). On the command line, `farik connect` writes `Open <verification_uri> and enter the code <user_code>.` and the same warning sentence to the error stream, opens the page, waits ten minutes, and writes `Signed in to <Provider>.`.

Signing in with Google, for Farik's own connector (added in 0.59; ADR 0035's amendments of 2026-10-05 and 2026-10-06, ADR 0042; phase 7 step 08e). Farik's Google app, a Desktop client in a Google Cloud project the founder owns and Farik Cloud holds from the web launch (changed in 0.61, ADR 0044), signs an agent in for Farik's own `google-ads` connector with the one scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords`; the connector itself is step 08f's, and until its name is among Farik's own connectors nothing matches the entry. `app_for_farik_connector(table, command, args)` answers an entry only when `command` and `args` are exactly `farik connector <name>` for one of Farik's own connectors and `<name>` is the entry's `farik_connector`; a `stdio` entry with `oauth` that no entry answers is `sign_in_not_supported`, as Google's is in every build, since no build has a Google entry (changed in 0.64). The entry's flow, `AppFlow::Loopback { authorization_endpoint }`, is Farik's own requests and not `rmcp`'s, since there is no MCP server whose metadata to discover: the listener of the sign-in above (`http://localhost:<free port>/callback`, `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]`, five tries, the first `GET /callback` with the attempt's `state`, a plain page that names the provider, ten minutes), a random `state`, a verifier of 64 characters and its S256 challenge, and an authorization address that carries exactly `client_id`, `redirect_uri`, `response_type=code`, `scope` (the entry's scopes joined by a space), `state`, `code_challenge` and `code_challenge_method=S256`, with no `resource`, since there is no MCP resource, and no `access_type`. The team file's `oauth.scopes`, when given, must each be among the entry's, else `sign_in_failed` ("Farik's <name> sign-in asks only for <the entry's scopes, joined by a comma and a space>") before any request; none means the entry's. The callback must carry an `iss` equal to the entry's issuer, else `sign_in_mismatch`; `iss` is required here, and step 08g's live check settles whether Google sends it; `error=access_denied` is `access_denied`. The exchange posts `code`, `client_id`, `client_secret`, `redirect_uri`, `grant_type=authorization_code` and `code_verifier`, asking for JSON, and is refused `sign_in_failed` when `token_type` is not `Bearer` (ASCII case ignored: "<name> did not give Farik a bearer token"), when there is no `refresh_token` ("<name> did not give Farik a lasting sign-in") and when the granted `scope` lacks one that was asked for ("you did not allow Farik all it asks of <name>; sign in again and tick every box"). The grant is step 03's with `app` the entry's `id`, the entry's issuer and token endpoint, the issuer as `resource` (bookkeeping, which nothing reads for an app's grant), no revocation endpoint and the granted scopes. A refresh of a grant of an app that has a `client_secret` sends it, looked up by `app` in the daemon's table (the command line's is `REGISTERED_APPS`) on each refresh and never kept in the grant, and sends no `resource`; a grant whose `app` the table lacks, as in every build before the web launch, has lapsed at once, with no request. Google's Testing status ends a refresh token after 7 days, which `invalid_grant` makes "Sign in again", and its consent screen warns that the app is unverified until Google has verified the scope, which waits for the web launch's website (ADR 0042's amendment). Remove deletes the local grant and cannot revoke it, since Google's revocation ends every agent's grant of the project at once; it says where to remove Farik, `https://myaccount.google.com/connections`.
